On the [territorial disputes in the South China Sea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Territorial_disputes_in_the_South_China_Sea "Territorial disputes in the South China Sea"), particularly around the [Natuna Islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natuna_Islands "Natuna Islands"), where China's [nine-dash line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nine-dash_line "Nine-dash line") overlaps with Indonesian [EEZ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Exclusive_economic_zone "Exclusive economic zone") claims, Jokowi stated that "there will be no compromise on sovereignty",[&#8202;](#ref-back-259-0)[\[259\]](#refnote-259) and renamed Indonesia's section of the [South China Sea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_China_Sea "South China Sea") the "North Natuna Sea".[&#8202;](#ref-back-260-0)[\[260\]](#refnote-260) In June 2016, he held a cabinet meeting off the islands aboard the [Indonesian Navy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesian_Navy "Indonesian Navy") corvette [KRI _Imam Bonjol_](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KRI_Imam_Bonjol_(383) "KRI Imam Bonjol (383)"), calling for increased maritime patrols in the area.[&#8202;](#ref-back-261-0)[\[261\]](#refnote-261) Under his administration, Indonesia also released an "Indo-Pacific Vision" for [ASEAN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Association_of_Southeast_Asian_Nations "Association of Southeast Asian Nations") countries, which treated the [Indian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_Ocean "Indian Ocean") and [Pacific Oceans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pacific_Ocean "Pacific Ocean") as a single interconnected geostrategic area and called for a regional architecture built on cooperation.[&#8202;](#ref-back-262-0)[\[262\]](#refnote-262) Indonesia further entered a trilateral agreement with Malaysia and the Philippines allowing coordinated patrols in the pirate-infested [Sulu Sea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sulu_Sea "Sulu Sea").[&#8202;](#ref-back-263-0)[\[263\]](#refnote-263)

In the Muslim world, Jokowi called on leaders at the [Organisation of Islamic Cooperation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Organisation_of_Islamic_Cooperation "Organisation of Islamic Cooperation") summit in [Jakarta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jakarta "Jakarta") to unite in support of reconciliation and [Palestinian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_of_Palestine "State of Palestine") independence.[&#8202;](#ref-back-264-0)[\[264\]](#refnote-264) Under Jokowi, Indonesia's foreign minister visited Palestine but rejected calls to establish bilateral diplomatic relations with Israel.[&#8202;](#ref-back-265-0)[\[265\]](#refnote-265) An [honorary consul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Honorary_consul "Honorary consul") was established in [Ramallah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ramallah "Ramallah") in the [West Bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Bank "West Bank"), though it had to be inaugurated in [Amman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amman "Amman"), Jordan.[&#8202;](#ref-back-FOOTNOTEWeatherbee2017173-0)[\[266\]](#refnote-FOOTNOTEWeatherbee2017173) Jokowi also condemned the [persecution of Rohingya Muslims](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rohingya_persecution_in_Myanmar_(2016%E2%80%93present) "Rohingya persecution in Myanmar (2016–present)") in [Myanmar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Myanmar "Myanmar") and oversaw the dispatch of four [Indonesian Air Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesian_Air_Force "Indonesian Air Force") transport planes carrying 34 tons of relief supplies for [Rohingya refugees in Bangladesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rohingya_refugees_in_Bangladesh "Rohingya refugees in Bangladesh").[&#8202;](#ref-back-267-0)[\[267\]](#refnote-267)[&#8202;](#ref-back-268-0)[\[268\]](#refnote-268) In October 2021, he advocated [COVID-19 vaccine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_vaccine "COVID-19 vaccine") equity, urging richer countries to share vaccines with poorer ones.[&#8202;](#ref-back-269-0)[\[269\]](#refnote-269)

In response to the [Russian invasion of Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine "Russian invasion of Ukraine"), Jokowi visited both countries in July 2022, meeting Ukrainian President [Volodymyr Zelenskyy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Volodymyr_Zelenskyy "Volodymyr Zelenskyy") and Russian President [Vladimir Putin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vladimir_Putin "Vladimir Putin") while emphasizing the need for [peace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peace_negotiations_in_the_Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine "Peace negotiations in the Russian invasion of Ukraine") and the restoration of global food supply chains.[&#8202;](#ref-back-270-0)[\[270\]](#refnote-270) Although the visits did not contribute directly to a peace settlement, Jokowi was praised for the effort as a mediator.[&#8202;](#ref-back-271-0)[\[271\]](#refnote-271)[&#8202;](#ref-back-272-0)[\[272\]](#refnote-272) As Indonesia hosted the [2022 G20 Summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_G20_Summit "2022 G20 Summit") later that year, Jokowi also invited Zelenskyy to attend,[&#8202;](#ref-back-273-0)[\[273\]](#refnote-273) while resisting calls to revoke Russia's invitation to the summit.[&#8202;](#ref-back-274-0)[\[274\]](#refnote-274)

During a press conference at the White House on 14 November 2023, Jokowi called for a ceasefire in the [Gaza war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_war "Gaza war"), "for the sake of humanity", and urged U.S. President [Joe Biden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joe_Biden "Joe Biden") to do more to end "atrocities" in the [Gaza Strip](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_Strip "Gaza Strip").[&#8202;](#ref-back-275-0)[\[275\]](#refnote-275) On 11 October 2024, two Indonesian [TNI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesian_National_Armed_Forces "Indonesian National Armed Forces") soldiers serving as [UNIFIL](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UNIFIL "UNIFIL") peacekeepers were injured by [IDF](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IDF "IDF") tank fire at the UNIFIL headquarters in [Naqoura](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naqoura "Naqoura"), in southern [Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lebanon "Lebanon").[&#8202;](#ref-back-276-0)[\[276\]](#refnote-276) In response, Widodo called for caution amid the apparent [escalation and widening of the Israel-Hamas War to Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_war#Israel%E2%80%93Lebanon_border "Gaza war").[&#8202;](#ref-back-277-0)[\[277\]](#refnote-277)

### New capital

By April 2019, it had become public that Widodo had decided in a cabinet meeting to move the [capital of Indonesia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capital_of_Indonesia "Capital of Indonesia") away from Jakarta to a location outside [Java](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Java "Java").[&#8202;](#ref-back-278-0)[\[278\]](#refnote-278) On 25 August 2019, it was announced that the new capital would be located in [Kalimantan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kalimantan "Kalimantan"), between the regencies of [Penajam North Paser](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Penajam_North_Paser "Penajam North Paser") and [Kutai Kartanegara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kutai_Kartanegara "Kutai Kartanegara").[&#8202;](#ref-back-279-0)[\[279\]](#refnote-279) On 30 July 2024, Jokowi began working in the new capital, [Nusantara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nusantara_(planned_city) "Nusantara (planned city)").[&#8202;](#ref-back-280-0)[\[280\]](#refnote-280)

## Post-presidency

Widodo's second term ended on 20 October 2024, and he was succeeded by [Prabowo Subianto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prabowo_Subianto "Prabowo Subianto"), with Jokowi's eldest son, [Gibran Rakabuming Raka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gibran_Rakabuming_Raka "Gibran Rakabuming Raka"), becoming vice president.[&#8202;](#ref-back-281-0)[\[281\]](#refnote-281) In the [2024 Indonesian local elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Indonesian_local_elections "2024 Indonesian local elections"), he publicly endorsed 84 candidates for the position of governor, mayor and regent.[&#8202;](#ref-back-282-0)[\[282\]](#refnote-282) Candidates backed by Jokowi performed strongly in the elections, notably in [Central Java](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Java "Central Java"), where his preferred candidate, [Ahmad Luthfi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ahmad_Luthfi "Ahmad Luthfi"), defeated the PDI-P-backed candidate, [Andika Perkasa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andika_Perkasa "Andika Perkasa"), in one of the party's traditional strongholds, and in [North Sumatra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Sumatra "North Sumatra"), where Jokowi's son-in-law [Bobby Nasution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bobby_Nasution "Bobby Nasution") defeated the incumbent governor.[&#8202;](#ref-back-283-0)[\[283\]](#refnote-283)

In April 2025, Widodo joined the advisory board of [Bloomberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bloomberg_News "Bloomberg News") New Economy. Other former senior officials on the board included the former Italian prime minister [Mario Draghi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mario_Draghi "Mario Draghi") and former United States secretary of commerce [Gina Raimondo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gina_Raimondo "Gina Raimondo").[&#8202;](#ref-back-284-0)[\[284\]](#refnote-284) He formally joined the [Indonesian Solidarity Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesian_Solidarity_Party "Indonesian Solidarity Party") (PSI) on 14 October 2025, though he only confirmed his membership in September 2026.[&#8202;](#ref-back-285-0)[\[285\]](#refnote-285)

## Personal life

See also: [Family of Joko Widodo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Family_of_Joko_Widodo "Family of Joko Widodo")

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2a/Marcoses_and_Jokowi_3.jpg/500px-Marcoses_and_Jokowi_3.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Marcoses_and_Jokowi_3.jpg) Joko Widodo and First Lady Iriana hosting Philippine president [Bongbong Marcos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bongbong_Marcos "Bongbong Marcos") at the [Bogor Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bogor_Palace "Bogor Palace") in Java on 5 September 2022

Widodo married his wife [Iriana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iriana "Iriana") on 24 December 1986. The couple have two sons and one daughter.[&#8202;](#ref-back-286-0)[\[286\]](#refnote-286) Their first son, [Gibran Rakabuming Raka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gibran_Rakabuming_Raka "Gibran Rakabuming Raka") (born 1 October 1987), studied in Sydney and Singapore, including at the Management Development Institute of Singapore (MDIS), and served as mayor of [Surakarta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Surakarta "Surakarta"), Jokowi's hometown, from 2021 to 2024. Their only daughter, Kahiyang Ayu (born 20 April 1991), completed an undergraduate degree in food technology at [Sebelas Maret University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sebelas_Maret_University "Sebelas Maret University") in Surakarta. Their second son, [Kaesang Pangarep](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaesang_Pangarep "Kaesang Pangarep") (born 25 December 1994), completed his secondary education at [ACS International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anglo-Chinese_School#Anglo-Chinese_School_(International)_Singapore "Anglo-Chinese School") in Singapore,[&#8202;](#ref-back-287-0)[\[287\]](#refnote-287) and is known mainly as an online vlogger. Jokowi has six grandchildren: a grandson and a granddaughter from Gibran (born in 2016 and 2019, respectively),[&#8202;](#ref-back-288-0)[\[288\]](#refnote-288) and a granddaughter, two grandsons from Kahiyang (born in 2018, 2020 and 2022, respectively) and one granddaughter from Kaesang (born in 2024).[&#8202;](#ref-back-289-0)[\[289\]](#refnote-289)[&#8202;](#ref-back-290-0)[\[290\]](#refnote-290)

Several members of Widodo's family later entered politics, running in the [2020 local elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Indonesian_local_elections "2020 Indonesian local elections"). Gibran declared his candidacy for the mayorship of Surakarta, as did Jokowi's son-in-law [Bobby Nasution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bobby_Nasution "Bobby Nasution") in [Medan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medan "Medan") and his brother-in-law Wahyu Purwanto in [Gunung Kidul Regency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gunung_Kidul_Regency "Gunung Kidul Regency").[&#8202;](#ref-back-291-0)[\[291\]](#refnote-291) Gibran and Bobby won their elections and both took office in 2021,[&#8202;](#ref-back-292-0)[\[292\]](#refnote-292) while Wahyu withdrew his candidacy at Jokowi's request.[&#8202;](#ref-back-293-0)[\[293\]](#refnote-293) Jokowi's younger sister Idayati married [Anwar Usman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anwar_Usman "Anwar Usman"), [Chief Justice of the Constitutional Court of Indonesia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chief_Justice_of_the_Constitutional_Court_of_Indonesia "Chief Justice of the Constitutional Court of Indonesia"), in May 2022.[&#8202;](#ref-back-294-0)[\[294\]](#refnote-294) In September 2023, Kaesang entered politics by joining [Indonesian Solidarity Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesian_Solidarity_Party "Indonesian Solidarity Party") and was named its chairman a few days later.[&#8202;](#ref-back-295-0)[\[295\]](#refnote-295) A month later, Gibran was announced as a vice-presidential candidate in the [2024 presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Indonesian_general_election "2024 Indonesian general election").[&#8202;](#ref-back-296-0)[\[296\]](#refnote-296)

Jokowi has been described as "Muslim but broadly secular in his outlook."[&#8202;](#ref-back-297-0)[\[297\]](#refnote-297) His 2019 statement that [religion and politics should be separated](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Separation_of_church_and_state "Separation of church and state") prompted public debate over whether he was promoting secularism in Indonesia.[&#8202;](#ref-back-298-0)[\[298\]](#refnote-298) In June 2013, a film titled _Jokowi_, depicting his childhood and youth, was released.[&#8202;](#ref-back-299-0)[\[299\]](#refnote-299) He objected to the film, saying that he considered his life too simple to merit a screen adaptation.[&#8202;](#ref-back-300-0)[\[300\]](#refnote-300)

According to _[The Economist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Economist "The Economist")_, Jokowi "has a penchant for loud rock music" and once owned a bass guitar signed by [Robert Trujillo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Trujillo "Robert Trujillo") of the heavy metal band [Metallica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metallica "Metallica"), which was later confiscated by the KPK.[&#8202;](#ref-back-301-0)[\[301\]](#refnote-301) In November 2017, Danish Prime Minister [Lars Løkke Rasmussen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lars_L%C3%B8kke_Rasmussen "Lars Løkke Rasmussen"), during an official visit to Jakarta, gave Jokowi a Metallica _[Master of Puppets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Master_of_Puppets "Master of Puppets")_ vinyl box set as a [diplomatic gift](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diplomatic_gift "Diplomatic gift"). It was signed by the band's drummer and co-founder, [Lars Ulrich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lars_Ulrich "Lars Ulrich"), who is Danish.[&#8202;](#ref-back-302-0)[\[302\]](#refnote-302) In keeping with his transparency policy, Jokowi paid IDR 11 million ($800) from his personal funds to keep the record after it had been declared a state asset, thereby avoiding accusations of gratification.[&#8202;](#ref-back-303-0)[\[303\]](#refnote-303) In support of [Megadeth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Megadeth "Megadeth")'s 2018 tour in Indonesia, the band's lead vocalist [Dave Mustaine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dave_Mustaine "Dave Mustaine") invited Jokowi to attend the concert.[&#8202;](#ref-back-304-0)[\[304\]](#refnote-304) Unable to attend, Jokowi instead opened the concert with a video greeting, saying, "I am a fan of Megadeth, I like their songs; [Sweating Bullets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sweating_Bullets_(song) "Sweating Bullets (song)"), [Ashes in Your Mouth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ashes_in_Your_Mouth "Ashes in Your Mouth"), and [Wake Up Dead](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wake_Up_Dead "Wake Up Dead"). Enjoy watching everyone".[&#8202;](#ref-back-305-0)[\[305\]](#refnote-305) He is also a fan of other metal bands, including [Lamb of God](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lamb_of_God_(band) "Lamb of God (band)"), [Carcass](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carcass_(band) "Carcass (band)") and [Napalm Death](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Napalm_Death "Napalm Death").[&#8202;](#ref-back-306-0)[\[306\]](#refnote-306) On 2 November 2013, while serving as governor of Jakarta, he was seen attending the rock festival [Rock in Solo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rock_in_Solo "Rock in Solo") in casual dress.[&#8202;](#ref-back-307-0)[\[307\]](#refnote-307)

Jokowi has often been noted for his resemblance to former U.S. president [Barack Obama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barack_Obama "Barack Obama"),[&#8202;](#ref-back-308-0)[\[308\]](#refnote-308)[&#8202;](#ref-back-309-0)[\[309\]](#refnote-309) and his outsider political profile has likewise invited comparisons with Obama.[&#8202;](#ref-back-310-0)[\[310\]](#refnote-310)[&#8202;](#ref-back-311-0)[\[311\]](#refnote-311)

Jokowi is a [silat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silat "Silat") practitioner. He has practised the Setia Hati Terate style of [Persaudaraan Setia Hati Terate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Persaudaraan_Setia_Hati_Terate "Persaudaraan Setia Hati Terate") since junior high school and later attained the first-degree _[pendekar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pendekar "Pendekar") warga_ rank on 16 November 2013.[&#8202;](#ref-back-312-0)[\[312\]](#refnote-312)[&#8202;](#ref-back-313-0)[\[313\]](#refnote-313)[&#8202;](#ref-back-314-0)[\[314\]](#refnote-314)

## Controversies

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fe/Unbalanced_scales.svg/120px-Unbalanced_scales.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail) | This ["criticism" or "controversy" section](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Criticism#Avoid_sections_and_articles_focusing_on_criticisms_or_controversies "Wikipedia:Criticism") **may compromise the article's [neutrality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Neutral_point_of_view#Article_structure "Wikipedia:Neutral point of view")**. Please help [integrate negative information](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Structure "Wikipedia:Structure") into other sections or remove [undue focus on minor criticisms and controversies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:BALASP "Wikipedia:BALASP")._(September 2026)_ |

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/dd/2024_Indonesian_local_election_law_protests%2C_22_August_2024%2C_Jeromi_Mikhael_85.jpg/500px-2024_Indonesian_local_election_law_protests%2C_22_August_2024%2C_Jeromi_Mikhael_85.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:2024_Indonesian_local_election_law_protests,_22_August_2024,_Jeromi_Mikhael_85.jpg) A [tape art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tape_art "Tape art") that reads " _DPR kacung Jokowi_" (lit.'House of Representatives is Jokowi's lackey') and criticism of Jokowi's nepotism during [2024 Indonesian local election law protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Indonesian_local_election_law_protests "2024 Indonesian local election law protests").

### Allegations of neo-authoritarianism

Jokowi was criticised for policies seen as weakening opposition parties, restricting [freedom of speech](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_of_speech "Freedom of speech"), and enabling repressive conduct by law enforcement officials during demonstrations.[&#8202;](#ref-back-HARTO-0)[\[315\]](#refnote-HARTO) Other policies, such as the [Omnibus Law on Job Creation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Omnibus_Law_on_Job_Creation "Omnibus Law on Job Creation") and the Electronic Information and Transaction Act (UU ITE), were also cited in support of these accusations.[&#8202;](#ref-back-316-0)[\[316\]](#refnote-316) The charge was rejected by the [PDI-P](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesian_Democratic_Party_of_Struggle "Indonesian Democratic Party of Struggle") secretary-general [Hasto Kristiyanto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hasto_Kristiyanto "Hasto Kristiyanto"), who argued that the government was responding to unlawful acts such as damaging public facilities or spreading fake news, and that the actions of the security forces therefore should not be seen as authoritarian repression.[&#8202;](#ref-back-317-0)[\[317\]](#refnote-317) Following the passage of several controversial bills and security crackdowns on major protests since 2019,[&#8202;](#ref-back-318-0)[\[318\]](#refnote-318) his government was criticised as exhibiting "neo-authoritarianism", a characterization linked by critics to the [New Order](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Order_(Indonesia) "New Order (Indonesia)") under Suharto.[&#8202;](#ref-back-HARTO-1)[\[315\]](#refnote-HARTO)[&#8202;](#ref-back-319-0)[\[319\]](#refnote-319)[&#8202;](#ref-back-Demokrasi.co.id-1)[\[177\]](#refnote-Demokrasi.co.id)[&#8202;](#ref-back-CNN_Indonesia-1)[\[178\]](#refnote-CNN_Indonesia)[&#8202;](#ref-back-Banyan-1)[\[179\]](#refnote-Banyan) The _[South China Morning Post](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_China_Morning_Post "South China Morning Post")_ described him as "Little Suharto".[&#8202;](#ref-back-little2-0)[\[320\]](#refnote-little2)

### Controversial appointments

Jokowi was strongly criticised after appointing [HM Prasetyo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muhammad_Prasetyo "Muhammad Prasetyo") as [attorney general](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Attorney_General's_Office_of_Indonesia "Attorney General's Office of Indonesia"), as Prasetyo was seen as lacking sufficient experience in the prosecutor's office and as being closely tied to a political party.[&#8202;](#ref-back-321-0)[\[321\]](#refnote-321) He was also seen as inconsistent when he appointed [Airlangga Hartarto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Airlangga_Hartarto "Airlangga Hartarto"), the general chairman of [Golkar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golkar "Golkar"), as [minister of industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Industry_(Indonesia) "Ministry of Industry (Indonesia)") after initially asking ministers not to hold concurrent positions as party administrators.[&#8202;](#ref-back-322-0)[\[322\]](#refnote-322) Previously, [Puan Maharani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Puan_Maharani "Puan Maharani") had also served for a time as chair of the PDI-P Central Committee while holding office as [Coordinating Minister for Human Development and Cultural Affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coordinating_Ministry_for_Human_Development_and_Cultural_Affairs "Coordinating Ministry for Human Development and Cultural Affairs").

Jokowi again drew widespread controversy and protests when he proposed Commissioner General [Budi Gunawan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Budi_Gunawan "Budi Gunawan") to the [DPR](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/House_of_Representatives_(Indonesia) "House of Representatives (Indonesia)") in mid-January 2015 as the sole candidate for [National Police Chief](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chief_of_the_Indonesian_National_Police "Chief of the Indonesian National Police"). Budi Gunawan was regarded by critics as having a poor record in the police and, as a former aide to [Megawati Sukarnoputri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Megawati_Sukarnoputri "Megawati Sukarnoputri"), his nomination was seen by some as an act of political repayment. A day before the DPR confirmed him as a candidate, he was named a suspect by the [Corruption Eradication Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corruption_Eradication_Commission "Corruption Eradication Commission") in an alleged fat bank account case. Jokowi then postponed his inauguration as National Police Chief until the legal process involving Budi Gunawan was completed, and appointed Deputy National Police Chief Commissioner General [Badrodin Haiti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Badrodin_Haiti "Badrodin Haiti") to carry out the office's daily duties.[&#8202;](#ref-back-323-0)[\[323\]](#refnote-323) [Badrodin Haiti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Badrodin_Haiti "Badrodin Haiti") was officially appointed National Police Chief on 17 April 2015,[&#8202;](#ref-back-324-0)[\[324\]](#refnote-324) and was later replaced by [Tito Karnavian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tito_Karnavian "Tito Karnavian"), while Budi Gunawan became head of [BIN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_Intelligence_Agency_(Indonesia) "State Intelligence Agency (Indonesia)").[&#8202;](#ref-back-:2-0)[\[325\]](#refnote-:2) Because of his decision not to proceed with Budi Gunawan's appointment as National Police Chief, Jokowi was jeered by [PDI-P](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesian_Democratic_Party_of_Struggle "Indonesian Democratic Party of Struggle") cadres.[&#8202;](#ref-back-326-0)[\[326\]](#refnote-326)

### Accusations of dynastic politics

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/69/2024_Indonesian_local_election_law_protests%2C_22_August_2024%2C_Jeromi_Mikhael_55.jpg/500px-2024_Indonesian_local_election_law_protests%2C_22_August_2024%2C_Jeromi_Mikhael_55.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:2024_Indonesian_local_election_law_protests,_22_August_2024,_Jeromi_Mikhael_55.jpg) A miniature [guillotine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guillotine "Guillotine") emblazoned with Jokowi's face and posters containing criticism and accusations of Jokowi's dynasty politics during the 2024 protests.

Jokowi faced accusations of [dynastic politics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dynastic_politics "Dynastic politics") and [nepotism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nepotism "Nepotism") toward the end of his second term.[&#8202;](#ref-back-kompasdynastic-0)[\[327\]](#refnote-kompasdynastic) These accusations arose after his eldest son, [Gibran Rakabuming Raka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gibran_Rakabuming_Raka "Gibran Rakabuming Raka"), ran as the vice-presidential candidate alongside [Prabowo Subianto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prabowo_Subianto "Prabowo Subianto") in the 2024 presidential election, following a Constitutional Court decision issued when the court was chaired by Jokowi's brother-in-law, [Anwar Usman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anwar_Usman "Anwar Usman"). The issue drew further attention as other members of the [Jokowi family](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Family_of_Joko_Widodo "Family of Joko Widodo") also entered or were linked to electoral politics, including [Bobby Nasution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bobby_Nasution "Bobby Nasution") in the North Sumatra gubernatorial election, [Kaesang Pangarep](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaesang_Pangarep "Kaesang Pangarep") as a possible successor to Gibran as mayor of Surakarta, and [Erina Gudono](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Erina_Gudono "Erina Gudono"), Kaesang's wife, who was said to be considering a run in the 2024 Sleman regental election.[&#8202;](#ref-back-328-0)[\[328\]](#refnote-328)[&#8202;](#ref-back-kompasdynastic-1)[\[327\]](#refnote-kompasdynastic) Jokowi was also accused of nepotism and of undermining "people's sovereignty".[&#8202;](#ref-back-329-0)[\[329\]](#refnote-329) _[Tempo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tempo_(Indonesian_magazine) "Tempo (Indonesian magazine)")_ magazine argued that Jokowi's dynastic politics were damaging democracy in Indonesia, and wrote that his interference in the [2024 Indonesian presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Indonesian_presidential_election "2024 Indonesian presidential election") was an attempt to protect himself from possible legal consequences.[&#8202;](#ref-back-330-0)[\[330\]](#refnote-330) The issue culminated in the [2024 Indonesian local election law protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Indonesian_local_election_law_protests "2024 Indonesian local election law protests") after the House of Representatives attempted to draft a regional elections ( _Pilkada_) bill that contradicted the [Constitutional Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitutional_Court_of_Indonesia "Constitutional Court of Indonesia")'s ruling.[&#8202;](#ref-back-331-0)[\[331\]](#refnote-331)

The [Organized Crime and Corruption Reporting Project](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Organized_Crime_and_Corruption_Reporting_Project "Organized Crime and Corruption Reporting Project") (OCCRP) included Jokowi among its finalists for the 2024 Person of the Year in Organized Crime and Corruption after he received one of the highest vote totals.[&#8202;](#ref-back-332-0)[\[332\]](#refnote-332) OCCRP said that "civil society groups and experts say that Jokowi's government significantly weakened [Indonesia's anti-corruption commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corruption_Eradication_Commission "Corruption Eradication Commission")" and that "Jokowi was also widely criticised for undermining Indonesia’s electoral and judicial institutions to benefit the political ambitions of his son, who is now vice-president."[&#8202;](#ref-back-333-0)[\[333\]](#refnote-333) OCCRP ultimately named the deposed Syrian president [Bashar al-Assad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bashar_al-Assad "Bashar al-Assad") as the winner.[&#8202;](#ref-back-334-0)[\[334\]](#refnote-334)

### Environmental issues

In 2019, during a presidential debate with [Prabowo Subianto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prabowo_Subianto "Prabowo Subianto"), Jokowi sought to win public support by highlighting Prabowo's alleged ownership of approximately 120,000 hectares of land in Aceh.[&#8202;](#ref-back-335-0)[\[335\]](#refnote-335) In 2020, Joko Widodo enacted the Omnibus Law (Job Creation Law), which requires palm oil entrepreneurs to support smallholder plantations in the name of _social justice_ for poor communities, while also creating opportunities to legalise previously illegal forest clearing.[&#8202;](#ref-back-336-0)[\[336\]](#refnote-336)

During the floods in Aceh, North Sumatra, and West Sumatra at the end of 2025,[&#8202;](#ref-back-337-0)[\[337\]](#refnote-337) Jokowi's volunteer team again targeted [Prabowo Subianto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prabowo_Subianto "Prabowo Subianto") over his land ownership in Aceh and accused [Susilo Bambang Yudhoyono](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Susilo_Bambang_Yudhoyono "Susilo Bambang Yudhoyono") of issuing the largest number of forest-release permits,[&#8202;](#ref-back-338-0)[\[338\]](#refnote-338) citing Greenpeace data.

However, there is no evidence that President [Susilo Bambang Yudhoyono](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Susilo_Bambang_Yudhoyono "Susilo Bambang Yudhoyono") issued permits for forest clearing on degraded or critical lands before 2014, nor is there evidence that Jokowi granted permits on critical lands that caused the floods in Aceh, West Sumatra, and North Sumatra.

### Rumors of interference

On 12 February 2024, investigative journalist [Dandhy Laksono](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dandhy_Laksono "Dandhy Laksono") released _Dirty Vote_, a documentary he directed and uploaded to YouTube, alleging that Joko Widodo had used state funds to support Prabowo Subianto's campaign. The film went viral within a day and prompted accusations of sabotage from Prabowo's campaign team.[&#8202;](#ref-back-339-0)[\[339\]](#refnote-339) The presidential office denied the claims, and protests were held in response to the allegations.[&#8202;](#ref-back-abc-0)[\[340\]](#refnote-abc)

### University diploma

Main articles: [Joko Widodo university diploma controversy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joko_Widodo_university_diploma_controversy "Joko Widodo university diploma controversy") and [2025 Indonesian protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Indonesian_protests "2025 Indonesian protests")

Jokowi's university diploma has been the subject of authenticity allegations. The first public figure to claim that Joko Widodo's academic degree was fake was Bambang Tri Mulyono in his book _Jokowi Undercover_. In 2017, he was tried in court and sentenced to three years in prison for defaming Joko Widodo.[&#8202;](#ref-back-341-0)[\[341\]](#refnote-341)

[Rismon Hasiholan Sianipar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rismon_Hasiholan_Sianipar?action=edit&redlink=1 "Rismon Hasiholan Sianipar"), an alumnus of the Faculty of Technology of [Gadjah Mada University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gadjah_Mada_University "Gadjah Mada University") (UGM) who believes Jokowi's diploma is fake, wrote a forensic analysis of the degree.[&#8202;](#ref-back-342-0)[\[342\]](#refnote-342) His work was supported by telematics expert and former Minister of Youth and Sports [Roy Suryo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roy_Suryo "Roy Suryo").[&#8202;](#ref-back-:4-0)[\[343\]](#refnote-:4) Suryo cited an earlier post of his from 25 February 2020, which included a page from a 1985 graduation book. He said the book showed a photograph of the late [Hari Mulyono](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hari_Mulyono?action=edit&redlink=1 "Hari Mulyono") under the name "Jokowi". Suryo also questioned the authenticity of Jokowi's diploma and highlighted the typefaces used in the document, arguing that printing technology in 1985 would not have allowed the fonts seen in Jokowi's diploma and thesis documents.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:4-1)[\[343\]](#refnote-:4)

According to allegations advanced by Rismon Sianipar, Roy Suryo, and Tifauzia Tyassuma, Jokowi attended UGM but did not graduate because his grade point average was below two,[&#8202;](#ref-back-344-0)[\[344\]](#refnote-344) while his brother-in-law Hari Mulyono did graduate. They further alleged that Joko Widodo borrowed and used Hari Mulyono's diploma for employment purposes and later used a diploma bearing Hari Mulyono's serial number, together with a photograph they described as a composite of Dumatno and Jokowi, as part of the requirements for his candidacy for Mayor of Surakarta. In 2017, Hari Mulyono reportedly died under unexplained circumstances.[&#8202;](#ref-back-345-0)[\[345\]](#refnote-345)[&#8202;](#ref-back-346-0)[\[346\]](#refnote-346) The police rejected these allegations and instead named Rismon Sianipar, Roy Suryo, and Dr. Tifauzia Tyassuma as suspects in cases of _defamation_ and _slander_.[&#8202;](#ref-back-347-0)[\[347\]](#refnote-347)

On 15 and 16 April 2025, protests over the authenticity of his university diploma took place at the UGM campus and at his residence in Surakarta.[&#8202;](#ref-back-348-0)[\[348\]](#refnote-348)[&#8202;](#ref-back-349-0)[\[349\]](#refnote-349)

On 22 May 2025, after comparing Jokowi's university diploma with other diplomas and UGM's university records, the [Criminal Investigation Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Criminal_Investigation_Agency "Criminal Investigation Agency") declared the diploma authentic.[&#8202;](#ref-back-350-0)[\[350\]](#refnote-350)[&#8202;](#ref-back-351-0)[\[351\]](#refnote-351)

## In popular culture

_[Jokowi Adalah Kita](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jokowi_Adalah_Kita?action=edit&redlink=1 "Jokowi Adalah Kita")\[ [id](https://id.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jokowi%20adalah%20Kita "id:Jokowi adalah Kita")\]_ is an Indonesian drama film based on Jokowi's life. The film stars Ben Joshua, Sylvia Fully, and Agustin Taidy, and was released on 20 November 2014.[&#8202;](#ref-back-352-0)[\[352\]](#refnote-352) Its broadcast, however, was delayed amid protests over the government's decision to raise fuel prices.[&#8202;](#ref-back-353-0)[\[353\]](#refnote-353)

One of his best-known quotes is "_Yo ndak tau. Kok tanya saya?_" (in [Indonesian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesian_language "Indonesian language"), usually shortened to YNTKTS), which literally means "I don't know. Why are you asking me?"[&#8202;](#ref-back-354-0)[\[354\]](#refnote-354) He first used it while serving as [Governor of Jakarta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Governor_of_Jakarta "Governor of Jakarta") in response to questions from journalists, and it became closely associated with his public manner.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:0-0)[\[355\]](#refnote-:0) The phrase became widely popular across social media in the 2020s.[&#8202;](#ref-back-356-0)[\[356\]](#refnote-356) In Indonesian [internet slang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Internet_slang "Internet slang"), it is often used jokingly or satirically in response to a problem.[&#8202;](#ref-back-357-0)[\[357\]](#refnote-357) A variation of the phrase was coined by his youngest son, [Kaesang Pangarep](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaesang_Pangarep "Kaesang Pangarep"), when journalist [Najwa Shihab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Najwa_Shihab "Najwa Shihab") asked about Jokowi's hobby of raising [tadpoles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tadpole "Tadpole") on her talk show. He replied, "_Ya tanya bapak. Jangan tanya saya._" (usually shortened to YTBJTS), meaning "Yeah. Ask my dad. Don't ask me."[&#8202;](#ref-back-:0-1)[\[355\]](#refnote-:0)

| [2012](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_Jakarta_gubernatorial_election "2012 Jakarta gubernatorial election") | [Governor of Jakarta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Governor_of_Jakarta "Governor of Jakarta") | 2,472,130 | 53.82% | 1st | —N/a | Won |  |  |
| [2014](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Indonesian_presidential_election "2014 Indonesian presidential election") | [President of Indonesia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President_of_Indonesia "President of Indonesia") | 70,997,833 | 53.15% | 1st | —N/a | Won |  |  |
| [2019](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Indonesian_presidential_election "2019 Indonesian presidential election") | 85,607,362 | 55.50% | 1st | +2.35 | Won |  |  |  |

### Acknowledgments

- **2008:** Listed by _[Tempo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tempo_magazine_(Indonesia) "Tempo magazine (Indonesia)")_ as one of the 'Top 10 Indonesian Mayors of 2008'.[&#8202;](#ref-back-370-0)[\[370\]](#refnote-370)
- **2012:** Ranked 3rd at the 2012 [World Mayor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Mayor "World Mayor") Prize for "transforming a crime-ridden city into a regional centre for art and culture and an attractive city to tourists".[&#8202;](#ref-back-371-0)[\[371\]](#refnote-371)
- **2013:** Nominated as the global mayor of the month by the _City Mayors Foundation_, based in London.[&#8202;](#ref-back-372-0)[\[372\]](#refnote-372)
- **2013:** Listed as one of "The Leading Global Thinkers of 2013" in _Foreign Policy_ _Magazine_.[&#8202;](#ref-back-373-0)[\[373\]](#refnote-373)
- **2014:** Listed by _[Fortune](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fortune_(magazine) "Fortune (magazine)")_ as one of "The World's 50 Greatest Leaders".[&#8202;](#ref-back-374-0)[\[374\]](#refnote-374)
- **2016–2017, 2024:** List by "The Muslim 500" as one of the most influential Muslims in the world, which ranked 11 in 2016 and 13 both in 2017 and 2024.[&#8202;](#ref-back-375-0)[\[375\]](#refnote-375)[&#8202;](#ref-back-376-0)[\[376\]](#refnote-376)[&#8202;](#ref-back-377-0)[\[377\]](#refnote-377)
- **2017:** Received the award for "Best Gratification Reporting" from the _[Corruption Eradication Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corruption_Eradication_Commission "Corruption Eradication Commission")_ _(KPK)_.[&#8202;](#ref-back-378-0)[\[378\]](#refnote-378)
- **2022:** Received "Certificate of Acknowledgement" from _[International Rice Research Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Rice_Research_Institute "International Rice Research Institute")_. IRRI has recognized Indonesia's resilient agricultural and food systems and rice self-sufficiency in 2019–2021.[&#8202;](#ref-back-379-0)[\[379\]](#refnote-379)
- **2022:** Received "Imam Hasan ibn Ali Peace Prize" from _[Abu Dhabi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abu_Dhabi "Abu Dhabi") Forum for Peace_.[&#8202;](#ref-back-380-0)[\[380\]](#refnote-380)
- **2022:** Received " [Global Citizen Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atlantic_Council_Global_Citizen_Awards "Atlantic Council Global Citizen Awards")" from _[Atlantic Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atlantic_Council "Atlantic Council")_. The Global Citizen Award is an award for figures who are considered to have made great contributions to overcoming poverty, climate change and equality.[&#8202;](#ref-back-381-0)[\[381\]](#refnote-381)
- **2023:** Received the title of "Father of Nusantara Unity and Culture" from the _Indonesian Christian Youth Movement (GAMKI)_.[&#8202;](#ref-back-382-0)[\[382\]](#refnote-382)
- **2024:** Received the award as the "Father of Indonesian Construction". The award was given by the _Indonesian National Construction Implementation Association (Gapensi)_.[&#8202;](#ref-back-383-0)[\[383\]](#refnote-383)
- **2024:** Received "FAO Agricola Medal" from _[Food and Agriculture Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Food_and_Agriculture_Organization "Food and Agriculture Organization")_ for making great progress in transforming Indonesia's agrifood system within the context of sustained economic growth, even in the face of global challenges and uncertainties, including the COVID-19 pandemic.[&#8202;](#ref-back-384-0)[\[384\]](#refnote-384)
- **2024:** Awarded the title of "Honorary Citizen" by the [_Mobile Brigade Corps (Brimob)_](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mobile_Brigade_Corps "Mobile Brigade Corps"), [Indonesian National Police (Polri)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesian_National_Police "Indonesian National Police").[&#8202;](#ref-back-385-0)[\[385\]](#refnote-385)
- **2024:** Awarded the honorary medal of public security and safety "Loka Praja Samrakshana" by the _Indonesian National Police (Polri)_ for his major role in the development of the Bhayangkara Corps organization.[&#8202;](#ref-back-386-0)[\[386\]](#refnote-386)

### Customary titles

- **2014:** Received the title "Ki Jaka Winata". This title was given by the _traditional community of [Garut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Garut_Regency "Garut Regency")_, [West Java](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Java "West Java").[&#8202;](#ref-back-387-0)[\[387\]](#refnote-387)
- **2015:** Received the customary title "Biji Nagara Madafalo" or Yang Dipertuan Agung Anak Negara from the _[Sultanate of Tidore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sultanate_of_Tidore "Sultanate of Tidore")_, [North Maluku](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Maluku "North Maluku").[&#8202;](#ref-back-388-0)[\[388\]](#refnote-388)
- **2016:** Crowned as [Dayak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dayak_people "Dayak people") King with the title "Raja Haring Hatungku Tungket Langit" by the _Dayak Customary Council_ (DAD) of [Central Kalimantan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Kalimantan "Central Kalimantan"), meaning wise king with a noble personality and supporter of national unity. This title serves as a token of appreciation from the Dayak people to President Joko Widodo, who is recognized as a capable leader of the Indonesian nation and a unifying force for national unity.[&#8202;](#ref-back-389-0)[\[389\]](#refnote-389)
- **2017:** Received the honorary customary title "Upu Kalatia Kenalean Da Ntul Po Deyo Routnya Hnulho Maluku" from the _people of [Maluku](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maluku_(province) "Maluku (province)")_ because he was considered a great leader who cared deeply about the welfare of the people's lives.[&#8202;](#ref-back-390-0)[\[390\]](#refnote-390)
- **2017:** Awarded the customary title "Kapiteng Lau Pulo" from the [_Tanah Bumbu_](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tanah_Bumbu_Regency "Tanah Bumbu Regency") _Customary Institution_, [South Kalimantan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Kalimantan "South Kalimantan").[&#8202;](#ref-back-391-0)[\[391\]](#refnote-391)
- **2018:** Given the customary name "Kambepit" with the title "Panglima Perang Adat Asmat" from the [_Asmat People_](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asmat_people "Asmat people").[&#8202;](#ref-back-392-0)[\[392\]](#refnote-392)
- **2018:** Received customary title "Tuanku Sri Indera Utama Junjungan Negeri" from _[Sultanate of Deli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sultanate_of_Deli "Sultanate of Deli")_.[&#8202;](#ref-back-393-0)[\[393\]](#refnote-393)
- **2018:** Received the award as "Pinisepuh" of the _Pasundan Association_.[&#8202;](#ref-back-394-0)[\[394\]](#refnote-394)
- **2018:** Received customary title "Rajo Balaq Mangku Nagara" from _[Komering people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Komering_people "Komering people")_, [South Sumatra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Sumatra "South Sumatra").[&#8202;](#ref-back-395-0)[\[395\]](#refnote-395)
- **2018:** Received customary title "Datuk Seri Setia Amanah Negara" from _[Riau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Riau "Riau") [Malay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malays_(ethnic_group) "Malays (ethnic group)") Customary Institution_.[&#8202;](#ref-back-396-0)[\[396\]](#refnote-396)
- **2019:** Received the title "Derayen Acang Aco" from the _traditional figure of Dayak Laundayeh_ in the [Krayan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Krayan "Krayan") region. Derayen Acang Aco means a great leader who is able to do and exert all efforts for the progress, welfare, and prosperity of his people.[&#8202;](#ref-back-397-0)[\[397\]](#refnote-397)
- **2022:** Received the customary title "Mosalaki Ulu Beu Eko Bewa" from the _Ende Community_, which means leader of the entire Indonesian region from [Sabang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sabang,_Aceh "Sabang, Aceh") to [Merauke](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Merauke_Regency "Merauke Regency").[&#8202;](#ref-back-398-0)[\[398\]](#refnote-398)
- **2022:** Awarded the Customary Honorary Title of the _[Sultanate of Buton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sultanate_of_Buton "Sultanate of Buton")_ "La Ode Muhammad Lakina Bhawaangi yi Nusantara".[&#8202;](#ref-back-399-0)[\[399\]](#refnote-399)
- **2022:** Crowned as Prince of the _[Sultanate of Ternate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sultanate_of_Ternate "Sultanate of Ternate")_ with the title "Kaitjil". Kaitjil was the prince or first line of the [Sultan of Ternate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_rulers_of_Maluku "List of rulers of Maluku").[&#8202;](#ref-back-:1-0)[\[400\]](#refnote-:1)
- **2022:** Awarded the title "Dada Madopo Malomo" by the [_Sultan of Ternate_](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_rulers_of_Maluku "List of rulers of Maluku") which means great leader.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:1-1)[\[400\]](#refnote-:1)
- **2023:** Received a customary title from the _Dayak tribe_ while visiting [West Kutai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Kutai_Regency "West Kutai Regency"), [East Kalimantan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Kalimantan "East Kalimantan"). The customary title received was "Ajiq Tatau Narakng Bulau, Penimakng Sookng Matiiq, Penerajuuq Bawe Ayaakng". The title has the meaning of having the right and authority to form and determine the best sons and daughters of the nation to carry out tasks aimed at peace and prosperity.[&#8202;](#ref-back-401-0)[\[401\]](#refnote-401)
- **2023:** Received customary title "Marambe Ambaralla Palunglaa Porodisa" from _[Talaud](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Talaud_Islands_Regency "Talaud Islands Regency") Customary Council_.[&#8202;](#ref-back-402-0)[\[402\]](#refnote-402)

- JKW Reservoir in [Nusantara Capital City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nusantara_(city) "Nusantara (city)").[&#8202;](#ref-back-403-0)[\[403\]](#refnote-403)

- Jokowi Iriana Park on Trikora Street, Kaimana City Subdistrict, Kaimana District, [Kaimana Regency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaimana_Regency "Kaimana Regency"), [West Papua](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Papua_(province) "West Papua (province)").[&#8202;](#ref-back-412-0)[\[412\]](#refnote-412)
